Copyright Guidelines

Latest update 25th April 2023

At Ko-fi, we are committed to fostering a creative and supportive environment that encourages the free expression of our creators, while also respecting the intellectual property rights of others.

These copyright guidelines outline the rules and guidelines for using copyrighted material on our service, to protect both creators and copyright holders.

Ko-fi’s Role in Copyright Matters

Ko-fi helps maintain a responsible service for creators and copyright holders but isn’t the ultimate authority in resolving disputes. If you face a copyright issue, you may need to communicate directly with the other party, seek legal counsel, and pursue legal channels if needed. While we handle DMCA notices and counter-notices, our involvement is limited.

By using Ko-fi, you acknowledge that resolving copyright disputes is primarily the involved parties’ responsibility. Ko-fi doesn’t provide legal advice nor do we possess expertise in validating copyright claims. As a user, you’re responsible for complying with copyright laws and seeking legal assistance when necessary.

Creator’s Rights and Responsibilities

Original Content

As a creator, you are responsible for ensuring that the content you share on Ko-fi is your original work or that you have obtained the necessary permissions to use any copyrighted material in your content.

Attribution

If you use copyrighted material in your content, you must provide proper attribution to the original creator or copyright holder. This includes but is not limited to citing sources, linking to the original work, and displaying the appropriate credits.

User-Generated Content

Please make sure any content you create or share, such as comments, forum posts, or interactions with other users, doesn’t infringe on anyone else’s intellectual property rights. While Ko-fi can monitor, review, or remove content, it’s not our responsibility to do so.

Fair Use

You may use copyrighted material in your content under the fair use doctrine, which allows for the limited use of copyrighted material without permission for purposes such as criticism, comment, news reporting, teaching, scholarship, or research. Examples of fair use may include quoting a passage from a book in a review or using a short clip from a movie for educational purposes. However, fair use is a complex legal concept and is determined on a case-by-case basis. If you have questions about whether your use of copyrighted material falls under fair use, please consult legal counsel before posting anything on Ko-fi.

Public Domain Works

Ko-fi allows creators to use works that are in the public domain, as defined by applicable copyright laws. Public domain works are not protected by copyright and may be freely used, reproduced, and distributed. Bear in mind, however, that rules governing public domain works can differ between countries, so you should be aware of the specific rules that apply to your jurisdiction.

If you wish to use a work that you believe is in the public domain, it is your responsibility to ensure that this is, in fact, the case. Here are some suggestions for verifying a work’s public domain status:

  • Check the date of publication: Works published before a certain year are generally considered to be in the public domain - this will vary by country.
  • Check the copyright status: You can search the copyright records of your country or region to determine whether a work is still under copyright protection.
  • Check for expired copyrights: Copyrights on works may expire after a certain number of years or if certain requirements are not met.
  • Check for creative commons licenses: Some creators may release their works under a Creative Commons license, which allows for certain uses of the work without requiring permission.
  • Consult legal counsel: If you have any doubts about the public domain status of a work, it is recommended that you consult with legal counsel.

Please note that even if a work is in the public domain, certain uses of the work may still be restricted by other laws or regulations. For example, a work may contain trademarked elements or may be subject to privacy or publicity rights.

Ko-fi does not take responsibility for the misuse of public domain works on our service, and we encourage all users to be mindful of their legal obligations when using such works.

Reporting copyright infringement

Report Process

If you believe that your copyrighted work has been used on Ko-fi without your permission, you can report this via the following steps:

  • Click the ellipsis (…) at the top right of a user’s Ko-fi profile that you want to report and then click ‘Report Abuse’. Next, pick ‘Copyright issue’ as the report reason and complete the rest of the form.
  • You can follow this same process for specific areas and content uploads on a Ko-fi page, including Gallery, Shop, Feed and Posts - simply navigate to the ellipsis (…) and click ‘Report Abuse’.

We take all reports of copyright infringement seriously and endeavour to assess these to the best of our ability based on the information and evidence supplied. In circumstances where we are unable to validate either a notice or counter-notice, we reserve the right to have both parties address the situation directly with each other through legal action and not through Ko-fi.

Forged DMCA Notices

Ko-fi takes the submission of DMCA notices very seriously. Any person who knowingly materially misrepresents that content or activity is infringing may be liable for damages, including costs and attorney’s fees, incurred by the party being impersonated, or by Ko-fi if we suffer damages as a result of such misrepresentation. If we determine that a DMCA notice submitted to us is forged, we will take appropriate action, which may include reporting the incident to the appropriate authorities.

Responding to Copyright Infringement Claims

Takedown Process

If Ko-fi can unequivocally prove the validity of the DMCA notice, Ko-fi will use our best endeavours to remove or disable access to the allegedly infringing content and notify the creator about this.

Counter-Notice

If you believe that your content was removed or disabled in error, you may submit a counter-notice to us by appealing any action that is taken by us as a result of a reported infringement.

A valid counter-notice should include:

  • Your signature (physical or electronic);
  • Identification of the material that has been removed or to which access has been disabled and the location at which the material appeared before it was removed or access to it was disabled;
  • A statement under penalty of perjury that you have a good faith belief that the material was removed or disabled as a result of a mistake or misidentification of the material to be removed or disabled;
  • Your name, address, and telephone number, and a statement that you consent to the jurisdiction of the Federal District Court for the judicial district in which your address is located, or if your address is outside of the United States, for any judicial district in which the service provider may be found, and that you will accept service of process from the person who provided notification of copyright infringement or an agent of such person; and
  • Evidence that you are the copyright owner of the content or have the right to act on behalf of the copyright owner.

If we receive a valid counter-notice, we may reinstate your content unless the copyright holder initiates legal action - if the original claimant wishes to challenge the restoration of the content, they may file a lawsuit seeking a court order to restrain the user from engaging in infringing activity.

Alternatively, the original claimant may submit a new DMCA notice if they believe that the restored content continues to infringe on their copyright.

Repeat Infringers

Ko-fi will, in appropriate circumstances, permanently unpublish users who repeatedly infringe on copyright. We reserve the right to define “repeat infringer” at our sole discretion and to determine whether to permanently unpublish based on factors such as the number of infringement claims, the nature of the content, and the user’s history on the service.

Modifications to these guidelines

As we continue to improve our service, we reserve the right to make updates to these guidelines without prior notice. You should check these guidelines regularly as your continued use of the service constitutes your acceptance of the revised guidelines.

Non-Compliance

By using Ko-fi, you agree to comply with our guidelines and understand that it is your responsibility to ensure that your content complies with copyright laws.

Here’s what may happen if you don’t comply with our copyright guidelines:

  • If we receive a valid infringement claim against your content or discover that your content violates our guidelines, we may remove or disable access to the content in question.
  • In some cases, we may temporarily unpublish your page to investigate potential copyright violations or to give you an opportunity to address any issues.
  • In the event of repeated infringements or severe violations of our copyright guideines, we may have to permanently unpublish your page/account. We reserve the right to determine when this action should be taken.

Further Questions

We understand that copyright law can be complex and confusing. If you have any questions or concerns about our copyright guidelines, please feel free to contact us.

DAC7 and UK reporting guide

DAC7 and its UK equivalent are a set of rules that affect how services like Ko-fi report information about certain creators and their sales to tax authorities.

If you live in the EU or UK and use Ko-fi to sell physical items or offer personal services, and you meet certain thresholds, we must share details about you and your sales with the tax authority where you live.

Who do these rules apply to?

These rules apply if you’re a creator living in one of the 27 EU countries or the UK and you meet any one of these thresholds in a calendar year:

  • Completed 30 or more sales of physical items in the calendar year, or
  • Sold over €2,000 (or £1,700 in the UK) worth of physical items in the calendar year, or
  • Sold any personal services (for example, Commissions) within the year

We may contact you before the end of the year if your sales so far suggest you’ll reach a threshold. We only report your sales if you meet a threshold over the full calendar year.

What do you have to do?

If you reach a threshold, we’ll email you during the calendar year and ask you to complete a secure form at ko-fi.com/tax-report. The form takes about five minutes and asks for some basic information about you, plus your tax identification number (TIN), also called your tax number. For most UK individuals, this is your National Insurance number.

Please complete the form by December 31. You’ll need to complete it each year you meet a threshold. See “How to fill in the form” below.

How to fill in the form

Note

Don’t refresh the page or press back in your browser while filling in the form, or you’ll have to start again.

You can change the form’s language using Select Language at the top.

Before you start

You’ll need:

  • Your full legal name (or your business’s registered name)
  • Your date of birth
  • Your full primary residence address, including postal code
  • Your tax number (UK individuals: your National Insurance number). See “Which tax number do I need?”
  • Your VAT number, only if you’re VAT registered
  • Businesses: your company registration number
Account classification

Choose “Yes, I am an individual” if you sell as yourself. This includes sole traders, freelancers and self-employed people, even if you have a trading name.

Choose “No, I am not an individual” only if you sell through a registered company, partnership, charity or other organization.

Identification details
  • Name: your first and last name exactly as they appear on official documents, with a space between them. No titles (Mr, Ms), numbers, nicknames or Ko-fi page name.
  • Address: enter your full primary residence address, including your postal code. The Postal Code box isn’t marked as required, but your report needs it.
Tax information
  • VAT: if you’re not VAT registered, tick “VAT Number is not required or has not been issued”. Only enter a VAT number in this box. Your tax number goes in the tax residency section below.
  • Tax residency and tax number:
    • Choose the country where you pay tax.
    • Enter the tax number issued by that country’s tax authority.
    • If you pay tax in more than one country, use Add Additional Residency.
    • Tick “I confirm there are no other tax residencies beyond what’s listed above”.
  • “TIN is not required or has not been issued”: almost everyone has a tax number, so only tick this if your country doesn’t issue you one. If you tick it, we may contact you to confirm why. See “I don’t have a tax number, or can’t find mine” in the FAQ.

Note

The UK tax number box says “National Insurance Number (NINO or UTR)”. If you chose “Yes, I am an individual”, enter your National Insurance number, even if you also have a UTR. Only use a UTR if you’re submitting for a business.

Summary

Check everything and use Edit to change anything before you press Submit. Dates are shown month first (MM/DD/YYYY).

Which tax number do I need?

  • UK: your National Insurance number (two letters, six numbers, one letter, e.g. QQ 12 34 56 C), even if you also have a UTR from Self Assessment. It’s on payslips, your P60 and in the HMRC app.
  • France: your numéro fiscal, 13 digits, shown on your tax notice.
  • Germany: your Steuerliche Identifikationsnummer (Steuer-ID), 11 digits.
  • Ireland: your PPS number, seven numbers followed by one or two letters.
  • Italy: your codice fiscale, 16 characters.
  • Netherlands: your BSN (burgerservicenummer), nine digits.
  • Spain: your NIF (eight numbers and a letter) or NIE (starts with X, Y or Z). Enter it without “ES” in front. It isn’t a VAT number.
  • Other EU countries: the personal tax number issued by your tax authority. Find your country’s tax number format on the European Commission’s website.

EU creators who don’t have a tax number will be asked for their place of birth instead.

I’m filling this in for a business

  • Name of entity: your registered business name, as on your company registration.
  • Business registration number:
    • UK: limited companies and LLPs, your Companies House registration number (eight characters).
    • EU: businesses, your national business register number.
  • Tax identification number:
    • Companies: your Companies House number.
    • Partnerships: your partnership UTR.
  • VAT number: enter it if your business is VAT registered.

What happens after you submit the form?

Once you press Submit, you’ll see a confirmation that your tax documentation is saved.

We check every form. If something is missing or doesn’t look right, we’ll email you and ask you to update it.

In January, we create a simple report showing you what information is being sent. You can view this report in your Ko-fi Account settings.

Reporting happens once a year. By January 31 each year, we combine all reports and send them to the tax authorities. If your sales don’t meet a threshold by the end of the year, your information won’t be sent.

What if you don’t complete the form?

We’ll give you as much time as we can to provide your information, and we’ll remind you by email. If our records show you meet a threshold, we’ll still share the information we have. If, after a series of attempts to retrieve the information, we don’t hear from you, we may be required to suspend access to your page.

Frequently asked questions

Do I need to complete a report?

These rules apply to creators who live in one of the 27 EU states or the UK and meet any of the thresholds. If you meet one, we’ll email you before the end of the year. There’s no need to contact us if you meet a threshold earlier in the year. We’ll let you know when it’s time.

Which countries do these rules cover?

DAC7 applies if you’re a resident of any of the 27 EU countries: Austria, Belgium, Bulgaria, Croatia, Cyprus, Czech Republic, Denmark, Estonia, Finland, France, Germany, Greece, Hungary, Ireland, Italy, Latvia, Lithuania, Luxembourg, Malta, Netherlands, Poland, Portugal, Romania, Slovakia, Slovenia, Spain, and Sweden.

UK reporting applies to UK-based creators.

As Ko-fi is based in the UK, some EU-based creators may also be reported to HMRC, which shares the information with their country’s tax authority.

What data will be collected and shared?

If you meet a threshold, we share your:

  • Name
  • Address
  • Date of birth
  • Country of tax residence
  • Income earned
  • Fees paid to Ko-fi
  • Tax identification number (TIN). For most UK individuals, this is your National Insurance number
  • Place of birth, if you live in the EU and don’t have a TIN
  • VAT number, if applicable
  • Company registration number, if you’re a business

Does Ko-fi deduct tax on my behalf?

No. Even if you meet a threshold, Ko-fi doesn’t withhold or deduct income tax, VAT, sales tax or any other taxes on your behalf. You’re responsible for managing your own tax obligations as set out in Ko-fi’s Terms of Service.

Is this a new tax?

No. DAC7 and the UK reporting rules don’t introduce any new tax or change how your income is taxed. The rules only cover what information services like Ko-fi are required to share with tax authorities.

I made an error when providing my information; can I update it?

Yes. You can update your information at ko-fi.com/tax-report. Please make any changes by December 31. If you have any issues, contact support.

If any of your details change, please update your information within 30 days.

I don’t have a tax number, or can’t find mine

EU: your tax authority’s website explains where to find yours. If your country doesn’t issue you a tax number, tick “TIN is not required or has not been issued” and contact us to tell us why.

UK: almost everyone in the UK has a National Insurance number. You can find yours on your payslips, your P60, in your personal tax account or in the HMRC app.

I have more than one Ko-fi page

We’ll email you about each Ko-fi page that meets a threshold. Please complete the form for each page we contact you about.

I am not resident in the EU or UK, does this apply to me?

No. If your primary residence isn’t in the EU or UK, these rules don’t apply to you.

If I deactivate my Ko-fi account, will my information be shared?

Yes. If you met a threshold during the year, we’re still required to report the information we have.

Where can I learn more about these rules?

Read the European Commission’s DAC7 guide.

Read the UK government’s reporting rules for digital platforms.

Learn about the OECD model reporting rules for digital platforms.

Do you still have questions?

We know tax rules can be confusing, so if you’re unsure, it’s best to talk to a local tax advisor. If you have questions about the reporting process on Ko-fi, feel free to create a support ticket, and we’ll do our best to help.

How do I report a page?

Seen something suspicious? 🕵️‍♀️ Help us keep Ko-fi safe. If you come across a page that seems to be breaking the rules, reporting it allows us to take action.

Here’s how to report a page:

There are a few different ellipsis (…) report options depending on what part of a Ko-fi page you’re on.

You can…

  • Click the ellipsis (…) at the top right of a user’s Ko-fi profile that you want to report and then click ‘Report Abuse’
  • You can do the same for specific areas and content uploads on a Ko-fi page, including Gallery, Shop, Feed and Posts - simply navigate to the ellipsis (…) and click ‘Report Abuse’.

When you make a report, please be sure to be as detailed as possible and, if applicable, include evidence.

As covered in our guidelines, the Ko-fi report form should not intentionally be misused to submit false claims, mass-target users, or report unrelated matters as specifically illegal.

Will the page owner know it was me?

No, we will only request your email address if you’re submitting a DMCA notice as the copyright owner which helps facilitate our investigation.

If you are a target of harassment, we’ll also give you a chance to send us your ko-fi page, for example: ko-fi.com/yourusername so that we can investigate your report.

When you submit a ticket, providing an email is necessary, but rest assured this is for internal use only and we will not disclose your identity to the reported page owner.

What should I report?

You can report issues associated with Ko-fi pages including:

  • Illegal content/activity
  • Scam or fake pages
  • Violent or hateful content
  • Prohibited adult or explicit content
  • Unauthorized use of your copyright-protected content

This list isn’t exhaustive, so make sure to check our guidelines below for more details.

Note

If your report involves not receiving an item or if there’s an issue with an unfulfilled commission, it’s best to have a look at our guide or submit a ticket to our support team.

What happens after I report a page?

Once we receive your report, our Trust and Safety team will review this and take the appropriate action.

If you choose to include your email address when submitting the report, you will receive an email informing you of the outcome of the report. This will only disclose details of the action taken, not the investigation process.

For reports concerning child sexual exploitation and abuse (CSEA)/child sexual abuse material (CSAM) specifically, you will not receive an email of the outcome.

Can I appeal a report’s outcome?

Yes, if you included your email address when submitting the report. If the email you receive informs you that our Trust and Safety team took no action on your report, you will have the option to appeal this decision. Our Trust and Safety team will then reassess the case and notify you of a final decision.

If a reassessment results in a user’s page or content being actioned, your details will not be disclosed to that user. We will never disclose the identity of anyone submitting any form of report or complaint.

What happens when I have more information but I have already submitted a report?

Please submit a new report and our Trust and Safety team will consider any additional evidence.

Alternatively, if no action is taken following your initial report and you wish to appeal this outcome, you can supply more information as part of that process.

Ko-fi Content Guidelines

Last updated 6th February 2024

Here at Ko-fi, we’re committed to creator freedom. We want you to be able to produce the content you want and share it with your fans and supporters. At the same time we also must make sure Ko-fi remains a safe place for everyone. Please take a moment to read these guidelines which must be followed by everyone using Ko-fi.

Read the full terms and conditions of use here.

1. Payment Provider Requirements

Without payment providers such as PayPal and Stripe, there can be no Ko-fi.

Ko-fi uses PayPal and Stripe for transactions. This means creators must stick to the rules set by PayPal and Stripe. If you’re getting payments through Ko-fi for something not allowed by your payment provider, we might need to Unpublish your Ko-fi account (i.e. page) temporarily or, in more serious or repeated cases, permanently.

To be clear, creators can’t use Ko-fi in ways that go against PayPal’s acceptable use policy or Stripe’s prohibited businesses terms. When you sign up for these services, you’re agreeing to their terms individually.

2. Scope of Guidelines, Web Linking Rules, and Offsite Conduct

2.1 These guidelines apply to all aspects of the Ko-fi service including but not limited to imagery, text, video, audio, services, physical items, and links located anywhere on Ko-fi including direct messages, galleries, profiles, posts, comments, commissions, shop, and any other location where content can be added to the service.

For the sale or offer of physical items, our Content Guidance on Physical Items article offers some further clarification - this information is additional; our main guidelines still apply.

2.2 Web Linking Rules

Links to/from Ko-fi:

We reserve the right to enforce our guidelines even if the violating content/activity occurs on an external page, profile or site linked to Ko-fi. This applies to both links to your Ko-fi page from other pages/profiles/sites and links from your Ko-fi page to other pages/profiles/sites. With the exception of Ko-fi pages that link from Ko-fi to illegal content/activity, we are not obligated to enforce our guidelines in this capacity but reserve the right to take the appropriate action on our service at our discretion.

Review of External Activities:

If we have verifiable evidence of your external content/activity and it being specifically connected to how your Ko-fi page is used, and this content/activity is not allowed on Ko-fi or by your payment provider, we may take action as stated. Verifiable evidence consists of information and/or data (such as screenshots, links, reports or other forms of documentation) that is reliable, credible and capable of supporting us in making a decision. If we decide to take action, this may include the Ko-fi page being temporarily unpublished until it aligns with our rules. In cases of serious or repeat violations, we may permanently unpublish or ban your page. If the external content/activity is primarily illegal, hateful, sexually explicit, or is in another way in violation of our guidelines, we may also request the removal of such links, irrespective of whether there is a financial connection between that content/activity and your Ko-fi page.

Prohibited Content Transactions:

Ko-fi should not be used to arrange the sale, purchase or transfer of prohibited content via other channels, such as third-party messaging services.

Limitation of Responsibility:

Ko-fi is ultimately not responsible for offences, behaviours, or posting activities elsewhere on the internet. As stated, if we find that external content/activity in breach of our guidelines has a direct connection to how a Ko-fi page is being used, we reserve the right but are not obligated to take enforcement action on our service except in cases where Ko-fi pages have linked from Ko-fi to illegal content/activity.

Right to Take Action in Severe Cases:

Furthermore, in cases of severe offsite conduct unrelated to Ko-fi (i.e., without links to or from Ko-fi), we also reserve the right to unpublish a Ko-fi page if we have verifiable evidence. This includes but is not limited to involvement in activities like Child Sexual Exploitation and Abuse (CSEA); distribution or solicitation of Child Sexual Abuse Material (CSAM); terrorist plots, recruitment, or other forms of violent extremism.

3. Prohibited Content or Uses of Ko-fi

Ko-fi pages and content must not be used in connection with any of the following:

3.1 Child Sexual Exploitation and Abuse (CSEA)/Child Sexual Abuse Material (CSAM)

Ko-fi reports CSEA/CSAM to the relevant authority/law enforcement

We treat CSEA/CSAM very seriously. If we find evidence of such content/activity on or associated with a Ko-fi page, this will result in an account ban and escalation to the authorities. This includes purchasing, requesting, or otherwise enquiring about CSAM.

We will also ban and report related activities such as grooming minors; sexting minors; and impersonation of minors including the use of images/videos of them to solicit payments.

Due to the seriousness of this area, where we have determined a potential risk based on the indicators present, we reserve the right to ban an individual on account of suspected activity.

For fictional variants of CSAM (e.g. loli/shota illustrations, 3D renders, AI-generated content), as well as any content that whatsoever sexualises children, it is important to recognise that these, too, are illegal in some countries. While we prioritise reporting CSAM/CSEA content that affects real children, we will still ban Ko-fi accounts if we find that fictional variants are posted on, linked to/from, and/or are being sold via our service.

3.2 All other forms of illegal content/activity
  • Terrorist and violent extremist content. This extends to support, promotion, glorification, or praise of terrorist groups, acts or ideology; raising financial support for terrorist groups; grooming and recruitment; incitement to violence; planning or threatening to, or joking about, committing an act of terrorism/extreme violence
  • Hate speech/offences - See section 3.3 for more information
  • Aiding or promoting self-harm or suicide
  • Threats to kill
  • Controlling or coercive behaviour
  • Public order offences, harassment, stalking, abuse, and fear or provocation of violence - See section 3.6 for more information on sexual harassment, specifically
  • Unlawful supply or offer to supply drugs, psychoactive substances and/or articles on how to these. This extends to purchase, attempted purchase or enquiry about purchasing
  • Firearms and other weapons - Selling, buying, or stating intent to sell or buy real weapons, along with their parts, accessories or ammunition
  • Assisting/involvement in unlawful immigration or human trafficking (in the context of Ko-fi, this includes fundraising for that purpose)
  • Foreign interference
  • Sexual exploitation of adults (causing or inciting prostitution for gain)
  • Possession of extreme pornography
  • Disclosing, or threatening to disclose, private sexual images and films with intent to cause distress
  • Proceeds of crime (i.e. use of illegal funds/assets). This includes hiding, getting, using, holding, or helping someone get these assets
  • Fraud - See section 3.8 for more information
  • Financial services violations (e.g. trading when not allowed; falsely saying you’re allowed to trade; not following rules about promoting financial services; making misleading statements or claims in finance)
  • Any involvement in planning or trying to do any of the illegal acts listed above. This includes planning or trying to commit these acts; urging, helping, or telling someone else to do them; supporting, advising, or getting involved in these acts in any way
  • Animal abuse/cruelty (note: this does not include circumstances where existing material is being used by organisations advocating against abuse/cruelty)
  • Swatting i.e. false reporting of a serious crime, resulting in a raid
3.3 Hate speech, harassment, intimidation, abuse and/or other offences targeting any individual, group, or institution

As stated, Ko-fi believes in freedom of expression but also respects the dignity of all individuals. Our hate speech policy is designed to create a balance between these principles and ensure Ko-fi remains a service where everyone feels welcome.

What is hate speech?

We define hate speech as any form of communication or content that threatens, insults, or discriminates groups or individuals based on any of the following:

  • Race, ethnicity, national origin, or skin colour
  • Religion, caste, or immigration status
  • Sex, sexual orientation, gender, or gender identity
  • Disability, serious conditions, diseases, or mental health disorders

Areas of hate speech:

  • Stereotyping and harmful generalisations
  • Generalisations and promotion of stereotypes intended to harm a group’s reputation and/or promote prejudice against them. For example, blaming an entire group for one person’s actions simply because of that person’s characteristics
  • Offensive language or visual representations
  • Language that offends, insults, or discriminates, including racial, religious, sexist, anti-LGBTQIA+ slurs, and culturally insensitive language, is prohibited. Degrading or mocking a person or group based on their characteristics through memes, artwork, photo alteration, and other media is also not allowed
  • Dehumanising speech
  • Content that dehumanises an individual or group, or suggests that they are inferior or unworthy of respect, is not allowed. This includes comparisons, generalisations, or statements that diminish their humanity (e.g. comparing a specific group to a plague)
  • Violent speech
  • We do not allow content that includes threats or promotes violence against individuals or groups
  • Segregation and exclusion We do not permit calls for segregation or exclusion based on protected characteristics

Educational and awareness content

We allow content that raises awareness or educates about hate speech, provided it does not promote or condone such speech.

3.4 Political or religious radicalism and other extreme views

We understand that everyone has their views on political and religious matters. However, we believe in maintaining a safe, respectful environment for all users. As such, we strictly prohibit political or religious radicalism and other extreme views, especially in the following scenarios:

  • Incitement to violence, vigilantism or vandalism
  • Calling for insurrection, rebellion, or any form of violent uprising against governments or institutions
  • Encouraging the doxing, harassment, or abuse of a specific individual or group
  • Plotting, glorifying, or advocating acts of terrorism or propagating terrorist ideology
  • Hate speech (this includes any form of hate speech directed towards individuals or groups based on race, ethnicity, gender, sexual orientation, religion, disability, or any other characteristic) - See section 3.3 for a more detailed explanation of our hate speech policy
  • Promotion of extremist ideologies
  • Fundraising or soliciting payments for extremist activities
  • Use of symbols, images, or language associated with extremist ideologies or groups
  • Harmful misinformation and conspiracy theories - See section 3.5 for more information
3.5 Harmful Misinformation

We recognise the dangers of harmful misinformation and prohibit it on or in connection with Ko-fi; this extends to any form of activity that seeks to promote or profit from this.

  • Political conspiracy theories with racist undertones or that stoke hatred/condemnation of a specific group (e.g. an ethnic or minority group)
  • Content that undermines regional stability, incites violence, glorifies ongoing acts of aggression, or promotes/supports state-sponsored violence
  • Denying the existence of a genocidal event, including the Holocaust
  • Anti-vaccine conspiracies and other medical falsehoods - note: participating in mainstream media discussion about the possible origins of COVID-19 is allowed. Claims about the vaccine that public health authorities have debunked and that may cause people harm due to their belief in them (e.g. refusing to get vaccinated) are not allowed
  • Promotion of harmful health practices that could pose a health risk
  • Potentially harmful claims about public figures or entities
  • Spreading false information about public safety events (e.g., natural disasters, terrorist attacks) that can cause panic or hinder emergency response efforts
  • Content that spreads false information about voting methods, election results, or the integrity of electoral processes
  • Deepfake content or other manipulated media intended to mislead or harm individuals or the public
3.6 Sexual Harassment

All forms of sexual harassment are prohibited on Ko-fi, including:

  • Cyberstalking
  • Non-consensual upskirt photography or groping content
  • Cyberflashing
  • Uninvited sexual remarks, advances or adult content-related requests
  • Sexist insults
3.7 Content relating to self-harm or suicide

Content that glorifies, encourages, or instructs on suicide and self-harm, including eating disorders, is prohibited. This includes targeted body stigma content or targeted depressive content designed to influence someone toward these actions. We also prohibit the promotion of dangerous stunts or challenges that are intended to encourage imitation or mass participation where there is an element of self-harm and/or risk of death.

We do make allowances for Creators to responsibly explore and depict this subject matter and related themes in their fictional artwork. We also take into consideration mainstream images from movies and other creative contexts that do not fall foul of the above.

In the event of real, non-fictional content, if we have reason to believe that the content involves the individual who has uploaded it, we will proactively remove this content to protect other users and reach out to that person with resources that may be of help to them. In severe cases, we may have to temporarily unpublish the person’s Ko-fi page for safety reasons.

Assisting someone to take their life is an illegal act - See section 3.2.

3.8 Fraudulent activity

This includes:

  • Money laundering or activity that violates any money laundering regulation
  • Using stolen cards to send money through Ko-fi
  • Chargeback fraud and ‘attacks’
  • Impersonating another person to solicit tips
  • Scamming e.g. collecting financial aid for stories of plight that have been proven false
  • Intentionally misleading Supporters about a service or content in exchange for tips

Where there is sufficient evidence with regard to suspected fraudulent activity or there are signs of irregular financial activity, your page may be temporarily unpublished until we verify this with you.

3.9 Other prohibited means of soliciting or sending payments

These include:

  • Coercion
  • Blackmail, including emotional blackmail
  • Threatening harm to oneself or others in exchange for money
  • Funding a ransom, human trafficking or exploitation, vigilantism, bribes or bounties
  • Transactions for the sale of items, goods or services including digital downloads with the purpose of avoiding paying taxes
  • Funding legal cases and challenges of any kind
  • Gambling-related activities including raffles, lotteries, giveaways or games of chance where a fee is paid to enter and winners are selected at random
  • Using payments as a form of harassment - if a payment is unwanted and has been refunded by the creator and/or they have blocked the sender, we will accept their report of harassment on these grounds
  • Impersonating an individual or organisation for financial gain
3.10 Doxing

Deliberate doxing i.e. sharing another person’s private details, such as their full name and address, contact info, private social media handle(s), and/or an aggregation of non-private information such as their name, photograph and city/town of residence, with the intention of harming their reputation and/or publicly exposing them is prohibited on Ko-fi.

Please respect everyone’s privacy, including that of Ko-fi staff.

3.11 Copyright infringement

This refers to products or services that directly infringe or facilitate infringement upon the trademark, patent, copyright, trade secrets, or proprietary or privacy rights of any third party.

Ko-fi may take action in clear-cut cases of widespread commercial copyright infringement, such as distributing pirated movies, selling unlicensed albums/books/audiobooks, or hosting large collections of translated media, even without a formal takedown request.

In other cases, especially where the rights and originality are disputed, we rely on formal copyright notices from the original rights holder or their appointed representative before taking action. For details on how to submit a valid notice, and to understand more about Ko-fi’s role in copyright matters, please head to our Copyright Guidelines.

3.12 Spamming

We have different approaches for different forms of spamming.

  • Commercial spamming i.e. using the Ko-fi service to boost the SEO ranking of a company or product unrelated to content creation and/or advertise on behalf of a client. For example, linking to or directly promoting gambling, spa, dentistry, medical, retail, and other third-party services. We do not allow SEO abuse and, particularly where our automated systems have identified efforts to mass-create Ko-fi accounts for this purpose, this will result in an immediate ban. Note that advertised services such as boosting (as defined in the context of online gaming), while loosely related to content creation, are also forbidden due to the potential compromise of users’ external accounts; being largely prohibited across the relevant industry; and potentially undermining the efforts of other creators e.g. gamers who stream in connection with Ko-fi.
  • Spam comments such as repeatedly posting comments on a user’s Ko-fi page that have been reported to us as unwanted by that user.
    • Concerning comments etiquette in the comments section of posts published by our official Ko-fi page, we will treat the following as spam:
      • Promotion of personal Ko-fi pages unless on topic. One-off comments will be deleted; repeated comments will result in that user being blocked by our official page.
      • One-word, offtopic comments (e.g. ‘hello’, etc.) - these will be deleted.
      • Promoting links to entirely unrelated products/services will result in temporary suspension (if found to be SEO abuse upon further investigation, this will instead result in a ban).
3.13 Violence and gore

Real-life extreme violence and/or gore, including violence-related illegal content, are not allowed on or in connection with Ko-fi (i.e. receiving payments for such content that is located elsewhere online).

Ko-fi allows moderate fictional gore and violence in most circumstances but prohibits convincingly real and/or gratuitously detailed content such as fake snuff footage and graphic depictions of torture.

For a more extensive list of examples of what is versus what isn’t allowed, see our NSFW Tag article. If you’re still unsure, contact us.

3.14 Adult content

Ko-fi offers an NSFW Tag option, but using this doesn’t mean that all kinds of NSFW content is allowed. Read more about using the tag here to see when it does/doesn’t apply.

Here is a list of adult-related content, services, and activities that we do not allow:

  • Sexual services (e.g. prostitution, escorts, pay-per-view, striptease/stripping, masturbation, adult chat/sexting, genital ratings, taking and fulfilling requests relating to any prohibited adult content in this list, etc.) For the avoidance of doubt, sexting includes euphemisms, visual and textual
  • Pornography, depictions of rape, incest, bestiality, and any other explicit adult or obscene content of a similar nature including implied, emulated, censored, and cropped versions across illustrative, video, audio content, etc. See our NSFW Tag article for further guidance on different content types.
  • Sexualised nudity (i.e. genitalia and areolae exposed in a way intended to be sexually provocative or arousing) including partial (i.e. partial exposure of genitalia and/or areolae). We recognise that nudity is also expressed non-sexually in cultural, medical, educational and/or artistic contexts and also consider the difference in risk and intent between fictional and real content. For detailed examples of what is versus what isn’t allowed, see our NSFW Tag article. If you’re still unsure, contact us
  • Offering uncensored versions of sexualised nudity or other prohibited adult content in this list to subscribers or at third-party sites or links in connection with Ko-fi. If we find evidence that money has been received for such content despite an absence of direct URL link(s) to/from Ko-fi, we may still take action in this context
  • Select uses of covering or censoring sexualised nudity and other prohibited adult content in this list. We consider various factors such as the extent to which the content is covered/censored; the severity of the content being covered/censored; and/or if the content is fictional artwork or real-life adult content creation. See our NSFW Tag article for detailed examples of what is versus what isn’t allowed. If you’re still unsure, contact us
  • High-risk fetish and paraphilia content, including findom (financial domination) themes. Factors we consider to distinguish between high-risk and low-to-no risk include how sexually explicit the content is, if at all; if the content is real or fictional; what harm it may pose to the health of participants and/or viewers; and/or whether the content has any basis in reality. See our NSFW Tag article for detailed examples of what is versus what isn’t allowed. If you’re still unsure, contact us
  • Providing, or activity related to the production of, sex simulators and other games solely or mainly focused on explicit adult content
  • Sale of other sexually explicit material, including physical items (e.g. sex toys, etc.).
3.15 Other content featuring minors

As a safety precaution, we prohibit certain content, events or activities involving minors or realistic AI visuals closely resembling minors which risk the sexualisation or exploitation of minors.

In particular, this includes child nudity even in non-sexual, non-abuse circumstances.

Factors we take into account are context, perceived intent, and if there is a risk that individuals with malicious intent may be drawn to and exploit the featured content/event/activity.

3.16 Underage users

If you are underage, your account will be unpublished until you turn 18 years of age and verify this with us through our verification steps. To do this, you should provide a well-lit, unobstructed photo of yourself holding legible government-issued ID and a slip of paper with your Ko-fi page name.

Successful verification will result in your account being republished.

However, in cases where the use of an account is further incompatible with our guidelines (e.g. connected to illegal content/activity), the account will remain unpublished and turning 18 years old will not change this outcome.

3.17 Content relating to drugs, alcohol, or harmful substances

The unlawful sale, offer or provision of drugs, psychoactive substances, and/or guidance on administering drugs/psychoactive substances is illegal - See Section 3.2.

We also do not permit the sale or purchase of drug paraphernalia or alcohol on Ko-fi. For content depicting, discussing or promoting drugs or alcohol, we take into account whether the content is real or fictional; the level of harm that the content may pose; and the type of drugs involved.

Furthermore, we prohibit content which encourages or instructs on interaction (e.g. ingesting/injecting/inhaling/etc) with any substance that is physically harmful or that becomes physically harmful through quantity.

Please see our NSFW Tag article for detailed examples of what is versus what isn’t allowed. If you’re still unsure, contact us.

3.18 Other content/activity harmful to Ko-fi and/or our Community

This includes, but is not limited to:

  • Content that slanders, libels, misinforms about or otherwise intentionally harms the reputation of Ko-fi, its affiliates, and other third parties
  • Fake reviews or intentionally misleading testimonials posted on your or someone else’s Ko-fi page about creators’ products or services.
  • Spreading spyware, malware, viruses or linking to malicious websites containing such links, software or bots
  • Attempting to compromise the Ko-fi service or individual accounts/pages on Ko-fi, or offering to compromise private accounts/profiles elsewhere online as a service provided via or in connection with Ko-fi
  • Exposing log in details for Ko-fi accounts/pages
  • Scraping data from the Ko-fi service
  • Re-selling or recirculating private content that has been purchased from another Creator/Supporter
  • Intentional misuse of Ko-fi’s reporting processes, including:
    • Submitting patently unfounded/false reports about another Ko-fi user
    • Encouraging or arranging mass reporting of another Ko-fi user
    • Knowingly using Ko-fi’s illegal content/activity report form for unrelated matters
3.19 Select AI-generated content uses

Content/activity found to be illegal/criminal may be reported to the relevant authority.

Using AI-generated content for any illegal purposes (e.g. creation of CSAM; identity theft; etc.) will result in an account ban and may be reported.

It is also against Ko-fi’s Terms to scrape content from the Ko-fi service - this includes scraping to train AI models.

The following uses concerning AI-generated content are also prohibited:

  • AI models that have been trained for prohibited adult content purposes
  • AI-generated content that uses the likeness of celebrities or public figures for NSFW purposes
  • AI-generated content which can be proven to have directly infringed on an artist’s copyrighted work

4. Other Reasons Why a Ko-fi Page Might Be Unpublished

Other reasons why we might unpublish a page

  • We sometimes need to do a verification check to understand why money is being received through a Ko-fi page. If your page is unpublished for this reason, you can demonstrate how you have received payments e.g. connect your Ko-fi page to the external profile/page/site through which you received money; upload or link to the content/services you provide if applicable; upload screenshots that show when your Ko-fi link was posted on an external profile/page/site; and/or, if you are fundraising, upload screenshots that show how your received payments are being used/spent. Verification is assessed at our discretion based on the information available, and we may not be able to republish your page if the evidence provided is incomplete, does not meet our standards, or if any related content, services, or activity is found to violate our guidelines.
  • If we have reason to believe that prohibited content is being provided offsite but through Ko-fi (e.g. being offered via Memberships), we may need to verify this with you - this is likelier to happen if a Creator has previously been unpublished for prohibited content. If your page is unpublished for this reason, please refer to the instructions in the above point.
  • You’ve used all three strikes on your page or pages - this means that any remaining or newly created Ko-fi pages will also be unpublished or banned as this will count as a permanent suspension. Or, similarly, you have already been permanently suspended on a previous page, whether due to 3/3 strikes or a separate reason.
  • If your Ko-fi page references (e.g. you’ve written in your bio text) a potentially prohibited content focus or other use of Ko-fi and we have evidence that you have tried to use Ko-fi for this purpose (e.g. our automated systems have prevented you from uploading related prohibited content), we may unpublish your page either temporarily or permanently (this depends on the intended content/use, its severity, etc.).
  • As part of our efforts to tackle fraud and spam, Ko-fi pages can also be banned as a result of automated rules. If you believe you have been wrongly unpublished in this regard, you can appeal to have that action/decision reviewed.

5. What if I Tag My Page as NSFW (Not Safe for Work)?

Ko-fi offers the ability to tag your page as NSFW, but this tag does not mean that a Ko-fi page can be used for prohibited content or uses detailed in these guidelines.

Find out more about what content is and is not allowed with the NSFW tag.

6. How Does Ko-fi Moderate Content?

6.1 Enforcement Approach

For content/activity on or in connection with Ko-fi, we endeavour to look at context, severity, and volume before deciding what, if any, action to take. If we do take action, this can range from temporary to permanent unpublishing (i.e., suspension) to a ban.

In cases of addressing harmful misinformation specifically (section 3.5), we consider whether:

  • The information has been disproved by numerous credible sources (e.g. health organisations, major news organisations, verified experts, and academic institutions)
  • The information has changed over time or is in a state of flux (e.g. what was once true is now false or vice versa)
  • The information is being shared with harmful intent or as a result of personal error or misunderstanding
  • The information is conveyed through provocative, incendiary language designed to stir up emotions

6.2 Moderation Methods

  • Reports: Anyone can report a Ko-fi page to us and we’ll investigate.
  • Automated systems: Ko-fi uses a variety of automated screening systems to help detect potentially harmful or prohibited content and prevent fraudulent transactions. This includes areas of the service not visible to the public, such as Direct Messages, to protect user safety and uphold our content guidelines. These systems are designed to flag high-risk content, not to read user conversations. Some content, including certain prohibited adult images or illegal content, may be automatically removed, while other content is sent for manual review. We supervise our automated rules and systems to detect and unpublish illegal content and actively update and track their performance to ensure they perform as accurately as possible.
  • Human moderators: Pages, content, and transaction activity are manually reviewed, reports investigated, and appeals processed daily by our dedicated Trust & Safety team.

6.3 Moderating Content Harmful to Children

While users must be 18 or older to use Ko-fi, we take any content that could be encountered by - and be harmful to - children seriously. We use a combination of automated systems, manual review, and user-submitted reports for the following content areas:

  • Pornographic content
  • Suicide content
  • Self-harm and self-injury content
  • Eating disorder content
  • Abuse content
  • Hate incitement content
  • Violence incitement content
  • Violent and graphic content
  • Animal abuse content
  • Bullying content
  • Harmful substance content
  • Dangerous challenges content (e.g. harmful viral trends)
  • Body stigma content
  • Depression content

7. Unpublished Accounts

When an account (i.e. page) is unpublished, it means that it has been suspended. In most cases, accounts are only temporarily unpublished and can be appealed. Find out more about appeals here.

In cases of severe or repeated violations, or otherwise complete unsuitability for the Ko-fi service per our guidelines, an account may instead be permanently unpublished.

Unpublished accounts can still be accessed/used by the account owner but will not be viewable to the public nor will it be possible to send Direct Messages (DMs) to other users.

8. Banned Accounts

Accounts (i.e. pages) on Ko-fi can also be banned. The difference between a banned and unpublished account is that a banned account, in addition to not being viewable to the public, cannot be accessed/used at all by the account owner.

We may ban an account due but not limited to mass SEO abuse (commercial spam); illegal content/activity (including suspected cases); or creating a new account after being permanently unpublished.

Bans can also be appealed via the same process as suspensions; see here for more information.

9. Strike System

Ko-fi runs a three-strike system which applies in most circumstances.

For example, uploading a prohibited image would result in strike one of three. Doing so again after the image has been removed would result in a second strike. A third time would then result in a third and final strike. At this point, your Ko-fi account (i.e. your page) becomes permanently unpublished.

To be clear, we do not issue a strike per piece of individual content at the same time. For example, if there are five prohibited pieces of content on a Ko-fi page, that’s still one strike on that occasion. But if more are uploaded after you have removed those, that would then be two strikes.

Content automatically removed by our systems will not count toward your strike total. Similarly, there may be other circumstances where a page has been unpublished for reasons unrelated to a violation e.g. for verification purposes.

The strike system applies even if you own multiple Ko-fi accounts (e.g. one strike per page still contributes toward a total of three strikes).

Accounts using Ko-fi in connection with illegal content/activity are not eligible for strikes and will instead be actioned immediately and permanently. We also reserve the right to take the same approach with other prohibited uses of Ko-fi, such as content deemed unsuitable for the service, based on the prevalence and/or severity of the content/activity/behaviour. If, after further review, we determine that a page’s overall use case or content is unsuitable for Ko-fi – whether due to newly discovered violations or additional context – we reserve the right to bypass the strikes system and take immediate, permanent action.

We encourage you to reach out to us if you’re unsure about your content/services being suitable for Ko-fi or if you have questions about the strikes system - we’re always happy to assist you.

10. Can I create a new page if I have been unpublished?

Sometimes, yes.

If your initial account (i.e. page) has been unpublished for a relatively minor reason (e.g. we just need you to remove a few images) but you decide you’d rather start over on a new account, that’s okay with us - remember that strikes do carry over to new pages, though.

If a Ko-fi account has been permanently unpublished either due to an overall unsuitable use of the service or as a result of having used up three strikes, or it has been banned, it is not possible to create a new account after that.

Ko-fi’s stance on AI

At Ko-fi, we’re here to support creators of all kinds - artists, writers, musicians, streamers, and more. Our goal is to provide tools that help you share your work and earn a living on your terms.

AI has become an increasingly significant topic in the creative world, bringing up questions about ethics, copyright, and fair use. We want to be transparent about where we stand, and the steps we’ve taken to protect creators on Ko-fi.

Protecting creators’ work

The community is at the heart of everything we do, and we believe creators deserve to have their work respected and protected. That’s why we do not support AI models being trained on creators’ work.

Ko-fi has always strictly prohibited the scraping of any content. This means AI bots and other automated tools are not allowed to collect content from our service. We’ve gone even further by using software to block major AI training bots, including OpenAI, Google, and Amazon, so that your content isn’t being scraped without your consent.

We can only control what happens on Ko-fi, so while we’re not able to protect content you share elsewhere, we’re committed to making sure your work is protected on our service.

Misrepresentation

Creators on Ko-fi are responsible for being honest about their work and how it was made. Presenting AI-generated content as your own human-made work to potential buyers goes against Ko-fi’s Terms and Conditions, which prohibit content that is misleading or dishonest. For example, marketing AI-generated reference images for commissions as hand-drawn, or selling AI-written content as your own writing.

If you believe a creator is misrepresenting their work in this way, you can report it for review here.

Why don’t you ban AI content on Ko-fi?

Ko-fi exists to help creators earn a living doing what they love, and the ways people create are more varied than ever. Rather than deciding which tools are acceptable, we focus on making sure all content on Ko-fi meets copyright law and our content guidelines. These guidelines reflect requirements and standards set by our payment providers, and apply equally to everyone.

Creators use a wide range of tools across their work, from assisted features within creative software, writing and music production, to accessibility tools like auto-captioning and image descriptions. We want to support that range of creativity without making judgement about individual creative choices.

Is AI content allowed in Ko-fi’s Discord and giveaways?

The Ko-fi Discord server is run by community volunteers and operates separately from the main service. Because AI discussions can become heated, the community has chosen not to allow AI content in the server to keep the space welcoming and focused on creator connections.

AI-generated work is not eligible for giveaways, challenges, or contests on Ko-fi, including those on our socials and in our Discord server.

We’re here to support you in doing what you love, and we’re committed to maintaining the integrity of your creative work. We’ll continue to listen to your feedback and adapt as the conversation around AI evolves. If you’d like to share your thoughts on AI and Ko-fi, you can do that here.

Policies FAQ

Here you’ll find our policies, content guidelines, and everything we do to help keep everyone safe on Ko-fi.

  • Terms - Understand the rules you agree to using Ko-fi.
  • Privacy policy - How we handle and protect your personal info.
  • Ko-fi content guidelines - What content is and isn’t allowed on Ko-fi, including our rules on linking.
  • Using the NSFW tag - Know when and how to use this tag.
  • Copyright guidelines - The guidelines for using copyrighted material on Ko-fi.
  • Content guidance on physical items - Learn what physical items you can and can’t offer.

We’re here if you need us. Feel free to send any questions our way. 💌
